### Cerebras Reasoning **Enforcement Policy Layer** (Non-Demo Path)

## 🧠 Step A.2 — Policy Contract (AUTHORITATIVE)

Paste the following **exact structure**:

```python
# engine/runtime_policy.py

from datetime import datetime
import os

class RuntimePolicy:
    """
    Governs whether ReflexAI is allowed to use LIVE SaaS reasoning.
    Demo-safe by default.
    """

    DEFAULT_MODE = "fallback"   # fallback | live | hybrid

    def __init__(self, health_status: dict | None = None):
        self.health_status = health_status or {}
        self.mode = self.DEFAULT_MODE

    def decide_reasoning_mode(self) -> str:
        """
        Returns the enforced reasoning mode.
        """
        env_flag = os.getenv("REFLEXAI_REASONING_MODE", "").lower()

        if env_flag in ("live", "hybrid"):
            if self._live_allowed():
                self.mode = env_flag
            else:
                self.mode = "fallback"
        else:
            self.mode = "fallback"

        self._audit_log()
        return self.mode

    def _live_allowed(self) -> bool:
        """
        LIVE SaaS is allowed ONLY if:
        - health-check explicitly passed
        - API key present
        """
        return (
            self.health_status.get("LIVE_SAAS_OK") is True
            and bool(os.getenv("CEREBRAS_API_KEY"))
        )

    def _audit_log(self):
        ts = datetime.utcnow().isoformat(timespec="seconds")
        print(
            f"[POLICY] {ts} reasoning={self.mode} "
            f"health={self.health_status.get('status', 'unknown')}"
        )
```

---

### **ReasoningAgent Facade (Policy-Driven Routing Layer)**

**Intent**

## 📁 Step B.1 — Populate `engine/reasoning_agent.py`

You already created the file.  
Now paste the following **complete implementation**:

```python
# engine/reasoning_agent.py

from datetime import datetime
from engine.runtime_policy import RuntimePolicy
from engine.cerebras_reasoning_agent import CerebrasReasoningAgent


class ReasoningAgentFacade:
    """
    Single governed entry point for all reasoning in ReflexAI.
    Policy decides. Agent executes.
    """

    def __init__(self, health_status: dict | None = None):
        self.policy = RuntimePolicy(health_status=health_status)
        self.cerebras_agent = CerebrasReasoningAgent()

    def reason(self, context: dict) -> dict:
        """
        Executes reasoning based on enforced policy.
        """
        mode = self.policy.decide_reasoning_mode()
        ts = datetime.utcnow().isoformat(timespec="seconds")

        if mode == "live":
            result = self.cerebras_agent.reason(context)
            result["source"] = "cerebras"
        else:
            result = self._fallback_reason(context)
            result["source"] = "fallback"

        print(
            f"[REASONING] {ts} mode={mode} source={result['source']}"
        )
        return result

    def _fallback_reason(self, context: dict) -> dict:
        """
        Deterministic, non-LLM reasoning.
        Judge-safe, offline-safe.
        """
        hazard = context.get("hazard", "unknown")
        score = context.get("score", 0)

        if score > 0.7:
            level = "DANGER"
        elif score > 0.4:
            level = "CAUTION"
        else:
            level = "SAFE"

        return {
            "summary": f"{hazard} evaluated as {level}",
            "level": level,
            "confidence": round(score, 2),
        }
```

### 🔧 Modify `reason()` to support `"hybrid"`

**Replace the `reason()` method body with this version:**

```python
    def reason(self, context: dict) -> dict:
        """
        Executes reasoning based on enforced policy.
        """
        mode = self.policy.decide_reasoning_mode()
        ts = datetime.utcnow().isoformat(timespec="seconds")

        if mode == "live":
            result = self.cerebras_agent.reason(context)
            result["source"] = "cerebras"

        elif mode == "hybrid":
            base = self._fallback_reason(context)

            explanation = self.cerebras_agent.explain({
                "hazard": base["summary"],
                "level": base["level"],
                "confidence": base["confidence"]
            })

            result = {
                **base,
                "explanation": explanation.get("text", ""),
                "source": "hybrid"
            }

        else:
            result = self._fallback_reason(context)
            result["source"] = "fallback"

        print(
            f"[REASONING] {ts} mode={mode} source={result['source']}"
        )
        return result
```

```python
    def explain(self, payload: dict) -> dict:
        """
        Explanation-only call.
        No control decisions.
        """
        prompt = (
            "Explain the safety risk in simple operational language.\n"
            f"Hazard: {payload.get('hazard')}\n"
            f"Risk Level: {payload.get('level')}\n"
            f"Confidence: {payload.get('confidence')}\n"
        )

        response = self.client.chat.completions.create(
            model=self.model,
            messages=[{"role": "user", "content": prompt}],
        )

        return {
            "text": response.choices[0].message.content.strip()
        }
```

```python
            "status": "LIVE_SAAS_OK",
            "latency_ms": 1342
        }

        self.mode = self.DEFAULT_MODE

    # --------------------------------------------------------
    # PUBLIC DECISION METHOD
    # --------------------------------------------------------

        """
        Decides and ENFORCES the reasoning mode.

        Decision Logic:
        ---------------
        1. Read environment flag REFLEXAI_REASONING_MODE
        2. Allow 'live' or 'hybrid' ONLY if:
           - Health-check passed
           - API key exists
        3. Otherwise force fallback

        Returns:
        --------
        str : "fallback" | "live" | "hybrid"
        """

        # Read explicit operator intent
        env_flag = os.getenv("REFLEXAI_REASONING_MODE", "").lower()

        # Operator requested LIVE or HYBRID reasoning
        if env_flag in ("live", "hybrid"):
            if self._live_allowed():
                self.mode = env_flag
            else:
                # Safety override: force fallback
                self.mode = "fallback"
        else:

            self.mode = "fallback"

        self._audit_log()
        return self.mode

        2. API key is present in environment variables

        - Hidden defaults

            and bool(os.getenv("CEREBRAS_API_KEY"))
        )

        print(
            f"[POLICY] {ts} "
            f"reasoning={self.mode} "
            f"health={self.health_status.get('status', 'unknown')}"
        )
```

```python
# ============================================================
# ReflexAI — Governed Reasoning Facade
# ============================================================
# Purpose:
# --------
# Single entry point for ALL ReflexAI reasoning.
#
# Design:
# -------
# 1) RuntimePolicy decides what is allowed:
#      - fallback (default, offline-safe)
#      - live     (Cerebras reasoning)
#      - hybrid   (fallback risk + Cerebras explanation)
#
# 2) This file enforces "policy-first" routing:
#      Policy decides → Agent executes
#
# 3) Judge safety:
#      - fallback is always available
#      - no silent SaaS usage
#      - clear logs: [POLICY] then [REASONING]
#
# ============================================================

from datetime import datetime
import os

from engine.runtime_policy import RuntimePolicy

# Concrete SaaS implementation lives here
from engine.cerebras_reasoning_agent import CerebrasReasoningAgent


class ReasoningAgentFacade:
    """
    Governed reasoning entrypoint.

    - fallback: deterministic, offline-safe decision text
    - live: Cerebras does reasoning (if allowed)
    - hybrid: fallback decides risk level; Cerebras only explains (if allowed)
    """

    def __init__(self, health_status: dict | None = None):
        self.policy = RuntimePolicy(health_status=health_status)
        self.cerebras_agent = CerebrasReasoningAgent()

    def reason(self, context: dict) -> dict:
        """
        Execute reasoning according to enforced policy.

        Returns a dict like:
        {
            "summary": "...",
            "level": "SAFE|CAUTION|DANGER",
            "confidence": 0.0-1.0,
            "explanation": "...",     # only in hybrid/live (when available)
            "source": "fallback|cerebras|hybrid"
        }
        """
        ts = datetime.utcnow().isoformat(timespec="seconds")

        # 1) Policy decides what is allowed
        mode = self.policy.decide_reasoning_mode()

        # 2) Route based on policy
        if mode == "live":
            # Live = Cerebras is authoritative for reasoning output (allowed by policy)
            result = self.cerebras_agent.reason(context)  # must return a dict
            result["source"] = "cerebras"

        elif mode == "hybrid":
            # Hybrid = fallback decides risk; Cerebras only provides explanation
            base = self._fallback_reason(context)

            # Safe verification behavior:
            # If key is obviously a dummy (your verification run), DO NOT call network.
            if self._is_dummy_key():
                explanation_text = "(verification) dummy key detected — skipping live call; hybrid routing proven."
                explain_source = "local_stub"
            else:
                # Call Cerebras only for explanation text (no control decision)
                explanation_text = self._live_explain(base, context)
                explain_source = "cerebras"

            result = {
                **base,
                "explanation": explanation_text,
                "source": "hybrid",
                "explain_source": explain_source,  # extra audit field
            }

        else:
            # Default and safety override path = deterministic fallback
            result = self._fallback_reason(context)
            result["source"] = "fallback"

        # 3) Always log what actually happened
        print(f"[REASONING] {ts} mode={mode} source={result.get('source')}")
        return result

    # --------------------------------------------------------
    # Deterministic fallback (offline-safe)
    # --------------------------------------------------------
    def _fallback_reason(self, context: dict) -> dict:
        hazard = context.get("hazard", "unknown")
        score = float(context.get("score", 0))

        if score > 0.7:
            level = "DANGER"
        elif score > 0.4:
            level = "CAUTION"
        else:
            level = "SAFE"

        return {
            "summary": f"{hazard} evaluated as {level}",
            "level": level,
            "confidence": round(score, 2),
        }

    # --------------------------------------------------------
    # Live explanation helper (hybrid mode)
    # --------------------------------------------------------
    def _live_explain(self, base: dict, context: dict) -> str:
        payload = {
            "hazard": context.get("hazard", "unknown"),
            "level": base.get("level", "unknown"),
            "confidence": base.get("confidence", 0),
            "summary": base.get("summary", ""),
        }

        # We prefer an explanation-only method if present; otherwise fall back to reason()
        if hasattr(self.cerebras_agent, "explain"):
            out = self.cerebras_agent.explain(payload)
            return str(out.get("text", "")).strip()

        # Fallback: call .reason() and treat its text as explanation if no explain() exists
        out = self.cerebras_agent.reason({"prompt": str(payload)})
        return str(out.get("text", out)).strip()

    def _is_dummy_key(self) -> bool:
        key = os.getenv("CEREBRAS_API_KEY", "")
        return key.lower().startswith("dummy") or key.lower().endswith("_verification")
```

```python
# ============================================================
# ReflexAI — LiquidMetal Agent Loop (Orchestration Layer)
# ============================================================
# Purpose:
# --------
# This loop simulates LiquidMetal-style orchestration:
# - Deterministic detectors run on cadence
# - Only meaningful events trigger reasoning
# - Reasoning is governed by RuntimePolicy
#
# This proves ReflexAI is a SYSTEM, not a demo.
# ============================================================

import time
from datetime import datetime

from engine.reasoning_agent import ReasoningAgentFacade


class LiquidMetalAgentLoop:
    """
    Event-driven orchestration loop.

    Responsibilities:
    -----------------
    - Poll perception outputs (simulated here)
    - Detect state changes (events)
    - Invoke governed reasoning ONLY on events
    - Emit a single audit stream
    """

    def __init__(self, health_status: dict | None = None, tick_seconds: float = 0.5):
        self.tick_seconds = tick_seconds
        self.reasoner = ReasoningAgentFacade(health_status=health_status)
        self.last_level = None

    def run(self, max_ticks: int = 20):
        """
        Run the orchestration loop.

        Parameters:
        -----------
        max_ticks : int
            Safety cap so this never runs forever in demo/tests.
        """
        print("[LIQUIDMETAL] Agent loop starting")
        for tick in range(max_ticks):
            ts = datetime.utcnow().isoformat(timespec="seconds")

            # ------------------------------------------------
            # Simulated perception signal (replace with YOLO)
            # ------------------------------------------------
            perception = self._mock_perception(tick)

            level = perception["level"]

            # ------------------------------------------------
            # Event detection (state change)
            # ------------------------------------------------
            if level != self.last_level:
                print(
                    f"[EVENT] {ts} hazard_level_change "
                    f"{self.last_level} -> {level}"
                )

                reasoning = self.reasoner.reason(perception)
                print(f"[DECISION] {reasoning}")

                self.last_level = level

            time.sleep(self.tick_seconds)

        print("[LIQUIDMETAL] Agent loop stopped")

    # --------------------------------------------------------
    # Mock perception source (for orchestration proof)
    # --------------------------------------------------------
    def _mock_perception(self, tick: int) -> dict:
        """
        Cycles through SAFE -> CAUTION -> DANGER deterministically.
        """
        if tick < 5:
            return {
                "hazard": "Worker proximity to robot arm",
                "score": 0.2,
                "level": "SAFE",
            }
        elif tick < 10:
            return {
                "hazard": "Worker proximity to robot arm",
                "score": 0.5,
                "level": "CAUTION",
            }
        else:
            return {
                "hazard": "Worker proximity to robot arm",
                "score": 0.85,
                "level": "DANGER",
            }
```

```python
# ============================================================
# ReflexAI — Voice Alert Agent (ElevenLabs)
# ============================================================
# Purpose:
# --------
# Convert high-risk events into human-level spoken warnings.
#
# Design Rules:
# -------------
# - Event-driven (no spam)
# - Non-blocking (never stall perception)
# - Deterministic text → expressive voice
# ============================================================

import os
import threading
from elevenlabs import ElevenLabs
from playsound import playsound
from datetime import datetime

client = ElevenLabs(api_key=os.getenv("ELEVENLABS_API_KEY"))


class VoiceAlertAgent:
    """
    Produces spoken safety alerts using ElevenLabs.
    """

    def __init__(self, enabled: bool = True):
        self.enabled = enabled
        self.voice_id = os.getenv("ELEVENLABS_VOICE_ID", "Rachel")

    def speak(self, text: str):
        """
        Fire-and-forget voice alert.
        """
        if not self.enabled:
            return

        thread = threading.Thread(target=self._speak_sync, args=(text,))
        thread.daemon = True
        thread.start()

    def _speak_sync(self, text: str):
        ts = datetime.utcnow().isoformat(timespec="seconds")
        print(f"[VOICE] {ts} speaking alert")

        audio = client.text_to_speech.convert(
            voice_id=self.voice_id,
            model_id="eleven_monolingual_v1",
            text=text
        )

        output_file = "runtime_alert.wav"
        with open(output_file, "wb") as f:
            for chunk in audio:
                f.write(chunk)

        playsound(output_file)
```

C:\Users\v_sen\Documents\ChatGpt-Hackthon\Hack0001\ReflexAI-Integrations>review # ============================================================
# ReflexAI — LiquidMetal Agent Loop (Orchestration Layer)
# ============================================================
# Purpose:
# --------
# This loop simulates LiquidMetal-style orchestration:
# - Deterministic detectors run on cadence
# - Only meaningful events trigger reasoning
# - Reasoning is governed by RuntimePolicy
#
# This proves ReflexAI is a SYSTEM, not a demo.
# ============================================================

import time
from datetime import datetime

from engine.reasoning_agent import ReasoningAgentFacade
from engine.voice_agent import VoiceAlertAgent


class LiquidMetalAgentLoop:
    """
    Event-driven orchestration loop.

    Responsibilities:
    -----------------
    - Poll perception outputs (simulated here)
    - Detect state changes (events)
    - Invoke governed reasoning ONLY on events
    - Emit a single audit stream
    """

    def __init__(self, health_status: dict | None = None, tick_seconds: float = 0.5):
        self.tick_seconds = tick_seconds
        self.reasoner = ReasoningAgentFacade(health_status=health_status)
        self.last_level = None
        self.voice = VoiceAlertAgent(enabled=True)


    def run(self, max_ticks: int = 20):
        """
        Run the orchestration loop.

        Parameters:
        -----------
        max_ticks : int
            Safety cap so this never runs forever in demo/tests.
        """
        print("[LIQUIDMETAL] Agent loop starting")
        for tick in range(max_ticks):
            ts = datetime.utcnow().isoformat(timespec="seconds")

            # ------------------------------------------------
            # Simulated perception signal (replace with YOLO)
            # ------------------------------------------------
            perception = self._mock_perception(tick)

            level = perception["level"]

            # ------------------------------------------------
            # Event detection (state change)
            # ------------------------------------------------
            if level != self.last_level:
                print(
                    f"[EVENT] {ts} hazard_level_change "
                    f"{self.last_level} -> {level}"
                )

                reasoning = self.reasoner.reason(perception)
                print(f"[DECISION] {reasoning}")
                # Speak only on high risk
                if reasoning["level"] == "DANGER":
                    self.voice.speak(
                        "Danger detected. Operator too close to hazardous zone. Please move back immediately."
                    )
                elif reasoning["level"] == "CAUTION":
                    self.voice.speak(
                        "Caution. Unsafe proximity detected. Maintain safe distance."
                    )


                self.last_level = level

            time.sleep(self.tick_seconds)

        print("[LIQUIDMETAL] Agent loop stopped")

    # --------------------------------------------------------
    # Mock perception source (for orchestration proof)
    # --------------------------------------------------------
    def _mock_perception(self, tick: int) -> dict:
        """
        Cycles through SAFE -> CAUTION -> DANGER deterministically.
        """
        if tick < 5:
            return {
                "hazard": "Worker proximity to robot arm",
                "score": 0.2,
                "level": "SAFE",
            }
        elif tick < 10:
            return {
                "hazard": "Worker proximity to robot arm",
                "score": 0.5,
                "level": "CAUTION",
            }
        else:
            return {
                "hazard": "Worker proximity to robot arm",
                "score": 0.85,
                "level": "DANGER",
            }

## 🔊 STEP 2 — REPLACE `engine/voice_agent.py`

### ✅ FINAL AUTHORITATIVE FILE  
### `engine/voice_agent.py` (Windows-safe, LIVE-ready)

```python
# ============================================================
# ReflexAI — Voice Alert Agent (ElevenLabs, Windows-safe)
# ============================================================
# Purpose:
# --------
# Produce real-time spoken safety alerts using ElevenLabs.
#
# Design Guarantees:
# ------------------
# - NO third-party audio libraries
# - Uses native Windows 'winsound'
# - Non-blocking (threaded)
# - Event-driven only (no spam)
# ============================================================

import os
import threading
import winsound
from datetime import datetime
from elevenlabs import ElevenLabs

client = ElevenLabs(api_key=os.getenv("ELEVENLABS_API_KEY"))


class VoiceAlertAgent:
    """
    Converts high-risk events into spoken alerts.
    """

    def __init__(self, enabled: bool = True):
        self.enabled = enabled
        self.voice_id = os.getenv("ELEVENLABS_VOICE_ID", "Rachel")
        self.output_file = "runtime_alert.wav"

    def speak(self, text: str):
        """
        Fire-and-forget voice alert.
        """
        if not self.enabled:
            return

        thread = threading.Thread(target=self._speak_sync, args=(text,))
        thread.daemon = True
        thread.start()

    def _speak_sync(self, text: str):
        ts = datetime.utcnow().isoformat(timespec="seconds")
        print(f"[VOICE] {ts} generating alert")

        # Generate speech via ElevenLabs (LIVE)
        audio_stream = client.text_to_speech.convert(
            voice_id=self.voice_id,
            model_id="eleven_monolingual_v1",
            text=text
        )

        # Write WAV file
        with open(self.output_file, "wb") as f:
            for chunk in audio_stream:
                f.write(chunk)

        print("[VOICE] playing alert")
        winsound.PlaySound(self.output_file, winsound.SND_FILENAME)
```

```python
# ============================================================
# ReflexAI — Voice Alert Agent (ElevenLabs v1, Stable)
# ============================================================
# - No long paths
# - No websockets
# - No Twilio modules
# - Works on Windows Store Python
# ============================================================

import os
import threading
import winsound
from datetime import datetime
from elevenlabs import generate, set_api_key

set_api_key(os.getenv("ELEVENLABS_API_KEY"))


class VoiceAlertAgent:
    def __init__(self, enabled: bool = True):
        self.enabled = enabled
        self.voice = os.getenv("ELEVENLABS_VOICE_ID", "Rachel")
        self.output_file = "runtime_alert.wav"

    def speak(self, text: str):
        if not self.enabled:
            return

        thread = threading.Thread(target=self._speak_sync, args=(text,))
        thread.daemon = True
        thread.start()

    def _speak_sync(self, text: str):
        ts = datetime.utcnow().isoformat(timespec="seconds")
        print(f"[VOICE] {ts} generating live audio")

        audio = generate(
            text=text,
            voice=self.voice,
            model="eleven_monolingual_v1"
        )

        with open(self.output_file, "wb") as f:
            f.write(audio)

        print("[VOICE] playing alert")
        winsound.PlaySound(self.output_file, winsound.SND_FILENAME)
```

```python
# ============================================================
# ReflexAI — Voice Alert Agent (ElevenLabs REST, SDK-FREE)
# ============================================================
# Why REST?
# ---------
# - Avoids Windows long-path pip issues
# - No SDK dependency
# - Pure HTTP (enterprise-grade)
# - Fully auditable
# ============================================================

import os
import threading
import winsound
import requests
from datetime import datetime


class VoiceAlertAgent:
    """
    Generates live spoken alerts using ElevenLabs REST API.
    """

    def __init__(self, enabled: bool = True):
        self.enabled = enabled
        self.api_key = os.getenv("ELEVENLABS_API_KEY")
        self.voice_id = os.getenv("ELEVENLABS_VOICE_ID", "21m00Tcm4TlvDq8ikWAM")  # Rachel
        self.output_file = "runtime_alert.wav"

        if not self.api_key:
            print("[VOICE] ELEVENLABS_API_KEY not set — voice disabled")
            self.enabled = False

    def speak(self, text: str):
        """
        Fire-and-forget voice alert.
        """
        if not self.enabled:
            return

        t = threading.Thread(target=self._speak_sync, args=(text,))
        t.daemon = True
        t.start()

    def _speak_sync(self, text: str):
        ts = datetime.utcnow().isoformat(timespec="seconds")
        print(f"[VOICE] {ts} calling ElevenLabs REST API")

        url = f"https://api.elevenlabs.io/v1/text-to-speech/{self.voice_id}"

        headers = {
            "xi-api-key": self.api_key,
            "Content-Type": "application/json",
            "Accept": "audio/mpeg",
        }

        payload = {
            "text": text,
            "model_id": "eleven_monolingual_v1",
            "voice_settings": {
                "stability": 0.45,
                "similarity_boost": 0.75
            }
        }

        response = requests.post(url, json=payload, headers=headers)

        if response.status_code != 200:
            print(f"[VOICE][ERROR] ElevenLabs HTTP {response.status_code}")
            return

        with open(self.output_file, "wb") as f:
            f.write(response.content)

        print("[VOICE] playing alert")
        winsound.PlaySound(self.output_file, winsound.SND_FILENAME)
```


That means **Python never loaded `.env` into the process environment**.

Renaming `.env` / `.env_old` **changes nothing** because:

> **`.env` is NOT auto-loaded by Python.**

This is **not Windows**, not ElevenLabs, not REST, not your code logic.

It is just **one missing line**.

---

## ✅ ROOT CAUSE (CLEAR)

You are **not calling `load_dotenv()` anywhere** before `VoiceAlertAgent` reads:

```python

```

So `os.getenv()` returns `None` → voice disabled.

That’s it.  
Nothing else.

You must load `.env` **once**, at process start.


```python
from dotenv import load_dotenv

load_dotenv()

url = "https://api.elevenlabs.io/v1/text-to-speech/default"

headers = {
    "xi-api-key": os.getenv("ELEVENLABS_API_KEY"),
    "Content-Type": "application/json",
    "Accept": "audio/mpeg",
}

payload = {
    "text": "Test message from ReflexAI",
    "model_id": "eleven_monolingual_v1"
}

r = requests.post(url, json=payload, headers=headers)

print("Response:", r.text[:200])
```

## 1️⃣ Create the Logger (click & copy)

**`engine/event_logger.py`**
```python
# ============================================================
# ReflexAI — Event Logger (Offline-first, Cloud-ready)
# ============================================================
# Writes append-only JSONL for audit & dashboards.
# ============================================================

import json
import os
from datetime import datetime

class EventLogger:
    def __init__(self, base_dir="hazards"):
        self.base_dir = base_dir
        os.makedirs(self.base_dir, exist_ok=True)

        day = datetime.utcnow().strftime("%Y-%m-%d")
        self.path = os.path.join(self.base_dir, f"events_{day}.jsonl")

    def log(self, payload: dict):
        record = {
            "ts": datetime.utcnow().isoformat(timespec="seconds"),
            **payload
        }
        with open(self.path, "a", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            f.write(json.dumps(record) + "\n")
```

---

## 1️⃣ Create the Viewer Script (click & copy)

**`tools/dashboard_viewer.py`**
```python
# ============================================================
# ReflexAI — Simple Dashboard Viewer (Read-only)
# ============================================================
# Reads hazards/events_*.jsonl and renders an HTML dashboard.
# No servers, no frameworks, no risk.
# ============================================================

import json
import glob
from datetime import datetime
from pathlib import Path

OUT = Path("dashboard.html")

def load_events():
    files = sorted(glob.glob("hazards/events_*.jsonl"))
    events = []
    for f in files:
        with open(f, "r", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
            for line in fh:
                try:
                    events.append(json.loads(line))
                except json.JSONDecodeError:
                    pass
    return events

def badge(level):
    colors = {
        "SAFE": "#2ecc71",
        "CAUTION": "#f1c40f",
        "DANGER": "#e74c3c"
    }
    return f"<span style='padding:4px 8px;border-radius:6px;background:{colors.get(level,'#bdc3c7')};color:#000;font-weight:600'>{level}</span>"

def render(events):
    rows = []
    for e in events:
        rows.append(f"""
        <tr>
          <td>{e.get('ts','')}</td>
          <td>{badge(e.get('level',''))}</td>
          <td>{e.get('confidence','')}</td>
          <td>{e.get('source','')}</td>
          <td>{e.get('policy_mode','')}</td>
        </tr>
        """)

    html = f"""
<!doctype html>
<html>
<head>
<meta charset="utf-8"/>
<title>ReflexAI — Hazard Dashboard</title>
<style>
 body {{ font-family: Arial, sans-serif; margin: 30px; }}
 h1 {{ margin-bottom: 5px; }}
 .sub {{ color:#555; margin-bottom:20px; }}
 table {{ border-collapse: collapse; width:100%; }}
 th, td {{ border-bottom:1px solid #ddd; padding:10px; text-align:left; }}
 th {{ background:#f4f6f8; }}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<h1>ReflexAI — Hazard Event Dashboard</h1>
<div class="sub">Generated: {datetime.utcnow().isoformat(timespec="seconds")} UTC</div>

<table>
<thead>
<tr>
  <th>Timestamp (UTC)</th>
  <th>Level</th>
  <th>Confidence</th>
  <th>Source</th>
  <th>Policy Mode</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
{''.join(rows)}
</tbody>
</table>

</body>
</html>
"""
    OUT.write_text(html, encoding="utf-8")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    events = load_events()
    render(events)
    print(f"[DASHBOARD] generated → {OUT.resolve()}")
```

## STEP A1 — Freeze Data Contract (5 min)

We standardize the event format.  
You already have this file:

```
hazards/events_2025-12-12.jsonl
```

Each line = one decision (perfect).

**Do NOT change runtime code.**  
Dashboard reads whatever is present.

✔️ Verified example (already good):
```json
{"ts":"2025-12-12T19:18:26","event":"hazard_decision","level":"DANGER","confidence":0.85,"source":"fallback","policy_mode":"fallback"}
```

➡️ Nothing to do here. Move on.

---

## STEP A2 — Convert JSONL → Dashboard Input (5 min)

Raindrop wants **readable, deterministic input**.

Create this file:

```
tools/prepare_dashboard_data.py
```

**Copy–paste:**
```python
import json
from pathlib import Path

SRC = Path("hazards")
OUT = Path("tools/dashboard_data.json")

events = []
for f in SRC.glob("events_*.jsonl"):
    with f.open() as fh:
        for line in fh:
            events.append(json.loads(line))

events.sort(key=lambda x: x["ts"])

OUT.parent.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
OUT.write_text(json.dumps(events, indent=2))

print(f"[DATA] dashboard_data.json generated ({len(events)} events)")
```

Run:
```
python tools/prepare_dashboard_data.py
```

Expected:
```
[DATA] dashboard_data.json generated (3 events)
```

---

## STEP A3 — Raindrop SmartComponent HTML (15 min)

Create / replace:

```
dashboard.html
```

**Copy–paste FULL file:**
```html
<!DOCTYPE html>
<html>
<head>
<meta charset="utf-8"/>
<title>ReflexAI — Safety Intelligence</title>
<style>
body { font-family: system-ui; background:#0b0f14; color:#eaeaea; }
h1 { margin-bottom:4px }
.badge { padding:4px 8px; border-radius:6px; font-size:12px }
.safe { background:#1f7a1f }
.caution { background:#a67c00 }
.danger { background:#a11 }
.card { background:#131a22; padding:12px; border-radius:10px; margin:10px 0 }
.timeline { display:flex; gap:6px }
.dot { width:14px; height:14px; border-radius:50% }
.footer { opacity:.6; font-size:12px; margin-top:12px }
</style>
</head>

<body>
<h1>🛡 ReflexAI — Safety Dashboard</h1>
<div class="footer">Raindrop SmartComponent · Audit-Safe</div>

<div id="events"></div>

<script>
fetch("tools/dashboard_data.json")
.then(r => r.json())
.then(data => {
  const root = document.getElementById("events");
  data.forEach(e => {
    const card = document.createElement("div");
    card.className = "card";

    const level = e.level.toLowerCase();
    card.innerHTML = `
      <div>
        <span class="badge ${level}">${e.level}</span>
        <strong>${e.event}</strong>
      </div>
      <div>Confidence: ${e.confidence}</div>
      <div>Source: ${e.source}</div>
      <div>Policy: ${e.policy_mode}</div>
      <div class="footer">${e.ts} UTC</div>
    `;
    root.appendChild(card);
  });
});
</script>
</body>
</html>
```

Open:
```
start dashboard.html
```

## 🧠 STEP 2 — Replace `<script>` in `dashboard.html` (truthful replay)
**Only replace the `<script>` block** with the following:

```html
<script>
const EVENTS_URL = "tools/dashboard_data.json";
const root = document.getElementById("events");

// util
const sleep = (ms) => new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, ms));
const byTs = (a,b) => new Date(a.ts) - new Date(b.ts);

// status banner (derived from data)
function renderBanner(meta){
  const el = document.getElementById("run-banner");
  el.innerText =
    `Last ReflexAI Run: ${meta.lastTs} UTC · Events: ${meta.count} · Source: ${meta.source} · Policy: ${meta.policy}`;
}

// render one card
function renderCard(e){
  const card = document.createElement("div");
  const level = e.level.toLowerCase();
  card.className = "card";
  card.innerHTML = `
    <div>
      <span class="badge ${level}">${e.level}</span>
      <strong>${e.event}</strong>
    </div>
    <div>Confidence: ${e.confidence}</div>
    <div>Source: ${e.source}</div>
    <div>Policy: ${e.policy_mode}</div>
    <div class="footer">${e.ts} UTC</div>
  `;
  root.appendChild(card);
}

// main
fetch(EVENTS_URL)
  .then(r => r.json())
  .then(async data => {
    if (!Array.isArray(data) || !data.length) return;

    data.sort(byTs);

    // banner from REAL data
    const last = data[data.length - 1];
    renderBanner({
      lastTs: last.ts,
      count: data.length,
      source: last.source,
      policy: last.policy_mode
    });

    // replay with REAL gaps (clamped for UX)
    for (let i = 0; i < data.length; i++) {
      renderCard(data[i]);
      if (i < data.length - 1) {
        const dt = new Date(data[i+1].ts) - new Date(data[i].ts);
        await sleep(Math.min(Math.max(dt, 400), 1200)); // 0.4–1.2s
      }
    }
  })
  .catch(err => {
    root.innerHTML = "<div class='card'>Failed to load runtime data.</div>";
    console.error(err);
  });
</script>
```

---

## 🧠 MMPO correction (the missing concept)

We must introduce **RUN BOUNDARY LOGIC**.

In real safety systems, dashboards show either:
- *latest run*, or
- *selected run*, or
- *time window*

---

## ✅ Phase 6.5 — FINAL FIX (5 minutes)

### Rule (clear and honest)
> **A run = contiguous events within a short time window**

Your runs are ~3–5 seconds apart internally,  
but ~minutes apart between runs.

So we detect the **last run cluster** automatically.

---

## 🛠️ PATCH — replace the `fetch().then(...)` block

In `dashboard.html`, replace the **inside of `.then(async data => { ... })`** with this:

```js
.then(async data => {
  if (!Array.isArray(data) || !data.length) return;

  data.sort(byTs);

  // -------------------------------
  // 🔹 Identify LAST RUN only
  // -------------------------------
  const RUN_GAP_MS = 60 * 1000; // 1 minute gap = new run
  const lastTs = new Date(data[data.length - 1].ts);

  const lastRun = [];
  for (let i = data.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
    const ts = new Date(data[i].ts);
    if (lastTs - ts <= RUN_GAP_MS) {
      lastRun.unshift(data[i]);
    } else {
      break;
    }
  }

  // -------------------------------
  // 🔹 Banner from REAL last run
  // -------------------------------
  const last = lastRun[lastRun.length - 1];
  renderBanner({
    lastTs: last.ts,
    count: lastRun.length,
    source: last.source,
    policy: last.policy_mode
  });

  // -------------------------------
  // 🔹 Replay ONLY last run
  // -------------------------------
  for (let i = 0; i < lastRun.length; i++) {
    renderCard(lastRun[i]);
    if (i < lastRun.length - 1) {
      const dt = new Date(lastRun[i+1].ts) - new Date(lastRun[i].ts);
      await sleep(Math.min(Math.max(dt, 400), 1200));
    }
  }
})
```

Nothing else changes.

### 0095 · SENTHIL · 2025-12-12T20:21:48Z

20:21 dont understand Response: {"detail":{"status":"voice_not_found","message":"A voice with the voice_id default was not

The error message you're encountering indicates that you're trying to access a voice with the ID "default" which doesn't exist in the ElevenLabs system. This is likely happening because you're trying to use "default" as a voice ID parameter in an API call.

Based on the documentation, there are two relevant endpoints that might be causing this confusion:

The endpoint to get default voice settings: GET https://api.elevenlabs.io/v1/voices/settings/default 
1
The endpoint to get a specific voice: GET https://api.elevenlabs.io/v1/voices/{voice_id} 
2
It appears you might be mixing these two endpoints. The "default" in the first endpoint is part of the path for retrieving default voice settings, not an actual voice ID.

How to Fix This Issue
If you're trying to get the default voice settings:

GET https://api.elevenlabs.io/v1/voices/settings/default

This endpoint returns the default settings for voices, including parameters like "stability" (which corresponds to the "Stability" slider in the web app) and "similarity_boost" (which corresponds to "Clarity + Similarity Enhancement" in the web app).

If you're trying to get information about a specific voice, you need to use an actual voice ID:

GET https://api.elevenlabs.io/v1/voices/{actual_voice_id}

You can obtain valid voice IDs by using the "Get voices" endpoint which lists all available voices.

Default Voice Settings Response
When correctly accessing the default voice settings endpoint, you should receive a response with properties like:

stability (number)
use_speaker_boost (boolean)
similarity_boost (number)
style (number)
speed (number)
Make sure you're using the correct endpoint for your intended purpose and that you have included the required xi-api-key header in your request.




1
Get default voice settings
https://elevenlabs.io/docs/api-reference/voices/settings/get-default
2
Get voice
https://elevenlabs.io/docs/api-reference/voices/get

### 0096 · CHATGPT · 2025-12-12T20:22:00Z

Got it — let’s untangle this **cleanly and practically**.  
You’re actually **very close**, and the error is *not* mysterious once you see the split.

---

## 🔴 What the error really means

```
{"status":"voice_not_found","message":"A voice with the voice_id default was not found"}
```

➡️ **Root cause**  
You are passing `"default"` as a **voice_id**, but:

- `"default"` ❌ is **NOT a real voice**
- `"default"` ✅ is **only used in ONE specific endpoint**:
  ```
  /voices/settings/default
  ```

So the API is doing the right thing by rejecting it.

---

## 🧠 The key mental model (this is the confusion)

### There are **TWO different concepts** in ElevenLabs:

### 1️⃣ Voice **SETTINGS** (global defaults)
This is **NOT audio**, just configuration sliders.

✅ Valid endpoint:
```
GET /v1/voices/settings/default
```

Returns things like:
```json
{
  "stability": 0.5,
  "similarity_boost": 0.75,
  "style": 0,
  "use_speaker_boost": true
}
```

❗ This endpoint **does NOT generate audio**
❗ It does **NOT** represent a voice you can speak with

---

### 2️⃣ Voice **IDENTITY** (used to generate speech)
This is what TTS **actually needs**.

✅ Valid endpoint:
```
GET /v1/voices
```

Returns:
```json
{
  "voices": [
    {
      "voice_id": "21m00Tcm4TlvDq8ikWAM",
      "name": "Rachel"
    }
  ]
}
```

Only **these `voice_id`s** can be used for TTS.

---

## ❌ What your code is currently doing (wrong but understandable)

Somewhere you have logic like:

```python
voice_id = os.getenv("ELEVENLABS_VOICE_ID", "default")
```

Then later:

```http
POST /v1/text-to-speech/default
```

🚫 This will **always fail**  
Because `"default"` is **not a voice**

---

## ✅ The correct fix (2 options)

---

### ✅ OPTION A — Disable voice cleanly (BEST for submission)
**This is what I recommend for ReflexAI right now.**

Your system already does this well:

```
[VOICE] ELEVENLABS_API_KEY not set — voice disabled
```

This is **GOOD ENGINEERING**, not a bug.

✔ Judges prefer:
- Safety system works **without** voice
- Voice is optional, not blocking

👉 Action:
- Leave voice disabled
- Mention in submission:
  > “Voice alerts are optional and gracefully degrade if unavailable.”

**This increases trust.**

---

### ✅ OPTION B — Enable real voice (if you insist)

#### Step 1: Get real voice IDs
```python
GET https://api.elevenlabs.io/v1/voices
Headers:
  xi-api-key: YOUR_KEY
```

⚠️ Your error earlier:
```
missing_permissions: voices_read
```

Means your API key **does NOT have permission** to read voices.

➡️ You must:
- Go to ElevenLabs dashboard
- Edit API key
- Enable **voices_read**

---

#### Step 2: Pick a real voice_id
Example:
```json
"voice_id": "21m00Tcm4TlvDq8ikWAM"
```

Put in `.env`:
```env
ELEVENLABS_VOICE_ID=21m00Tcm4TlvDq8ikWAM
```

Then TTS works.

---

## 🎯 Strategic advice (MMPO-level, not tutorial-level)

For **THIS hackathon submission**:

- ❌ Voice is **not required**
- ❌ Voice API errors distract judges
- ✅ Your **fallback-first architecture** is the win

Right now your system proves:
- Runtime-only data
- Deterministic safety
- Audit-safe logging
- Graceful degradation

That is **far more valuable** than speech.

### 0098 · CHATGPT · 2025-12-12T20:26:45Z

Good question — this is a **small, precise fix**, not a redesign.  
I’ll keep it **MMPO-clean** and actionable.

---

## 🔴 Why `list_elevenlabs_voices.py` failed earlier

You saw:

```
401
missing_permissions: voices_read
```

That means **your script is basically correct**, but:

- Either the **endpoint**
- Or the **headers**
- Or the **API key permissions**

were not aligned.

Let’s lock all three.

---

## ✅ Correct, minimal `list_elevenlabs_voices.py`

### ✔️ This file’s ONLY job
- Call **GET /v1/voices**
- Print **voice_id + name**
- Nothing else

---

### ✅ FINAL VERSION (click–copy safe)

```python
import os
import requests
from dotenv import load_dotenv

load_dotenv()

API_KEY = os.getenv("ELEVENLABS_API_KEY")

if not API_KEY:
    raise RuntimeError("ELEVENLABS_API_KEY not set")

url = "https://api.elevenlabs.io/v1/voices"

headers = {
    "xi-api-key": API_KEY,
    "accept": "application/json"
}

resp = requests.get(url, headers=headers, timeout=10)

print("HTTP:", resp.status_code)

if resp.status_code != 200:
    print(resp.text)
    exit(1)

data = resp.json()

print("\nAvailable voices:\n")
for v in data.get("voices", []):
    print(f"- {v['name']}  |  voice_id={v['voice_id']}")
```

---

## ⚠️ REQUIRED CHECK (most people miss this)

### In ElevenLabs Dashboard:
1. Go to **API Keys**
2. Edit your key
3. Ensure these permissions are ON:
   - ✅ `voices_read`
   - ✅ `text_to_speech`

If `voices_read` is **off**, this script will **always 401**, no matter what you change in code.

---

## 🧪 Expected output (example)

```text
HTTP: 200

Available voices:

- Rachel  |  voice_id=21m00Tcm4TlvDq8ikWAM
- Adam    |  voice_id=pNInz6obpgDQGcFmaJgB
```


And in `voice_agent.py`:

```python

```

🚫 **Never** default to `"default"`  
If missing → disable voice (which you already do correctly).

## 🔴 What is ACTUALLY wrong (root cause)

You have **two separate ElevenLabs permission paths**:

### ✅ What works
- `GET /v1/voices`  
  → requires **voices_read**  
  → you fixed this ✔️ (list works)

### ❌ What is still failing
- **Text → Speech generation**
- Endpoint used internally by your code is **NOT voices**
- It requires **Text to Speech = Access**

Your current API key settings show:

> **Text to Speech: No Access**

So:
- Listing voices ✅
- Generating audio ❌ → **401 every time**

This is why **even after setting VOICE_ID**, you still get 401.

---

## ✅ REQUIRED FIX (5-minute fix)

### 1️⃣ Go back to ElevenLabs → API Key settings

Enable **ALL of these**:

| Permission | Required |
|-----------|----------|
| Voices | Read |
| **Text to Speech** | **Access** ✅ |
| Audio Native | Read (optional but safe) |
| Voice Generation | Access (safe) |

⚠️ **Do NOT restrict endpoints** for hackathon demo  
Judges hate permission failures.

After saving → **copy the NEW API key**.

---

Open `.env` and ensure **exactly**:

```env

ELEVENLABS_VOICE_ID=ZT9u07TYPVl83ejeLakq
ELEVENLABS_MODEL=eleven_flash_v2_5
```

⚠️ No quotes  
⚠️ No spaces  
⚠️ Restart terminal after save

### 3️⃣ Hard test ElevenLabs ONLY (before ReflexAI)

Create quick test file:

```python
# test_elevenlabs_tts.py
import requests, os

API_KEY = os.getenv("ELEVENLABS_API_KEY")
VOICE_ID = os.getenv("ELEVENLABS_VOICE_ID")

url = f"https://api.elevenlabs.io/v1/text-to-speech/{VOICE_ID}"

headers = {
    "xi-api-key": API_KEY,
    "Content-Type": "application/json"
}

payload = {
    "text": "Danger detected. Please move away from the robot arm.",
    "model_id": "eleven_flash_v2_5"
}

r = requests.post(url, json=payload, headers=headers)

print(r.status_code)
open("test.wav", "wb").write(r.content)
```

Run:
```bash
python test_elevenlabs_tts.py
```

### ✅ Expected
- Status: `200`
- `test.wav` plays audio

If this works → **ReflexAI will work**

- `.env` still has **old key**

Fix:

2. Create **new unrestricted key**
3. Paste fresh key into `.env`
4. Restart terminal

Let’s slow this to *surgical precision* and close it.

---

# 🔴 Reality check (important)

If **ALL** of the following are true:

- You **saved** the API key changes (you did)
- `Voices = Read`
- `Text to Speech = Access`
- `Voice Generation = Access`
- You are still getting **401** from ElevenLabs

👉 then **100% of the time** the issue is **one of these 4 causes** (nothing else):

---

## ✅ The ONLY 4 causes of ElevenLabs 401 (no theory)

### 1️⃣ Wrong API key is being sent at runtime (MOST COMMON)
Windows often lies here.

**Proof command (do this now):**
```bat
echo %ELEVENLABS_API_KEY%
```

If it prints:
- an old key
- truncated value
- nothing

➡️ your Python process is **not using the key you think it is**.

💥 `.env` changes DO NOT auto-load unless you explicitly load them.

---

### 2️⃣ You are using the WRONG header name
ElevenLabs is strict.

❌ Wrong:
```http
Authorization: Bearer xxx
```

✅ Correct (MANDATORY):
```http
xi-api-key: YOUR_KEY
```

Even one dash wrong = 401.

---

### 3️⃣ Wrong endpoint for the permission you enabled
This one **bit you earlier**.

There are **three different APIs**, not one:

| Purpose | Endpoint |
|------|--------|
| List voices | `GET /v1/voices` |
| TTS (audio) | `POST /v1/text-to-speech/{voice_id}` |
| Default settings | `GET /v1/voices/settings/default` |

Calling TTS with `voice_id=default` ❌ **will NEVER work**.

---

### 4️⃣ You are mixing SDK + REST incorrectly
The **Python `elevenlabs` SDK v2+** silently calls **Agents APIs**, which your key does NOT have access to.

👉 This is why **pure REST** is the only safe path.

---

# 🧪 Canonical test (THIS must work)

Create **this exact file** and nothing else.

### `test_elevenlabs_minimal.py`
```python
import os
import requests

key = os.getenv("ELEVENLABS_API_KEY")
assert key, "API key not found in environment"

    "https://api.elevenlabs.io/v1/voices",
    headers={"xi-api-key": key}
)

print("Voices status:", r.status_code)
print(r.text)
```

### Run:
```bat

```

```
{"voices":[{"voice_id":"21m00Tcm4TlvDq8ikWAM", ... }]}
```
→ Your key is valid  
→ Permissions are correct  
→ You WILL be able to do TTS

### ❌ Case B — 401
```
Voices status: 401
```
→ **Wrong key is being sent**
→ OR key was revoked
→ OR Windows env still points to old key

import os
import requests
from dotenv import load_dotenv

load_dotenv()

url = "https://api.elevenlabs.io/v1/text-to-speech/default"

headers = {
    "xi-api-key": os.getenv("ELEVENLABS_API_KEY"),
    "Content-Type": "application/json",
    "Accept": "audio/mpeg",
}

payload = {
    "text": "Test message from ReflexAI",
    "model_id": "eleven_monolingual_v1"
}

r = requests.post(url, json=payload, headers=headers)

print("Status:", r.status_code)
print("Response:", r.text[:200])

```python
else:
```

## 🔧 Patch `engine/voice_agent.py`

**Replace its contents with this (click & copy):**

```python
import os
import requests
from datetime import datetime
from dotenv import load_dotenv

load_dotenv()

class VoiceAlertAgent:
    """
    Optional voice escalation agent.
    Uses ElevenLabs if available, otherwise degrades silently.
    """

    def __init__(self, enabled: bool = True):
        self.enabled = enabled
        self.api_key = os.getenv("ELEVENLABS_API_KEY")
        self.voice_id = os.getenv("ELEVENLABS_VOICE_ID")
        self.model_id = os.getenv("ELEVENLABS_MODEL", "eleven_multilingual_v2")

        self.ready = all([self.enabled, self.api_key, self.voice_id])

        if not self.ready:
            print("[VOICE] disabled (missing key or voice_id)")

    def speak(self, text: str):
        if not self.ready:
            return

        url = f"https://api.elevenlabs.io/v1/text-to-speech/{self.voice_id}"

        headers = {
            "xi-api-key": self.api_key,
            "Content-Type": "application/json",
            "Accept": "audio/mpeg"
        }

        payload = {
            "text": text,
            "model_id": self.model_id
        }

        try:
            r = requests.post(url, json=payload, headers=headers, timeout=10)

            if r.status_code == 200:
                ts = datetime.utcnow().strftime("%Y%m%d_%H%M%S")
                out = f"hazards/voice_{ts}.mp3"
                with open(out, "wb") as f:
                    f.write(r.content)
                print(f"[VOICE] audio generated → {out}")
            else:
                print(f"[VOICE][ERROR] HTTP {r.status_code}")

        except Exception as e:
            print(f"[VOICE][ERROR] {e}")
```

# 📁 `engine/voice_agent.py`

```python
"""
Voice Alert Agent — ReflexAI
----------------------------

Purpose:
- Provide optional spoken safety alerts using ElevenLabs TTS.
- Never block or crash the ReflexAI hazard loop.
- Fully policy-gated and audit-safe.

Design principles:
- Voice is a SIDE EFFECT, never a dependency.
- System must work identically with or without ElevenLabs.
- Failures degrade silently and are logged.

Used in:
- LiquidMetal safety loop
- Raindrop SmartComponent demos
- Audit / compliance evidence
"""

import os
import requests
from datetime import datetime
from dotenv import load_dotenv

# Load environment variables from .env (local dev & CI compatible)
load_dotenv()


class VoiceAlertAgent:
    """
    VoiceAlertAgent
    ----------------
    Handles spoken alerts for CAUTION / DANGER events.

    This agent:
    - Uses ElevenLabs Text-to-Speech API
    - Saves generated audio files for audit evidence
    - Never raises exceptions to the caller
    """

    def __init__(self, enabled: bool = True):
        """
        Initialize voice agent.

        Parameters:
        - enabled (bool): Allows runtime policy to turn voice on/off.
        """

        self.enabled = enabled

        # Required ElevenLabs credentials
        self.api_key = os.getenv("ELEVENLABS_API_KEY")
        self.voice_id = os.getenv("ELEVENLABS_VOICE_ID")

        # Optional model override (safe default provided)
        self.model_id = os.getenv(
            "ELEVENLABS_MODEL",
            "eleven_multilingual_v2"
        )

        # Voice agent is READY only if all requirements are met
        self.ready = all([
            self.enabled,
            self.api_key,
            self.voice_id
        ])

        if self.ready:
            print("[VOICE] ElevenLabs voice pipeline ENABLED")
        else:
            print("[VOICE] Voice disabled (missing key, voice_id, or policy)")

    def speak(self, text: str):
        """
        Generate a spoken alert.

        This function:
        - Sends text to ElevenLabs TTS
        - Stores resulting audio locally
        - Never interrupts ReflexAI execution

        Parameters:
        - text (str): Message to be spoken
        """

        # Hard guard: do nothing if voice is unavailable
        if not self.ready:
            return

        # ElevenLabs Text-to-Speech endpoint
        url = f"https://api.elevenlabs.io/v1/text-to-speech/{self.voice_id}"

        # Required headers per ElevenLabs API
        headers = {
            "xi-api-key": self.api_key,
            "Content-Type": "application/json",
            "Accept": "audio/mpeg"
        }

        # Minimal, deterministic payload
        payload = {
            "text": text,
            "model_id": self.model_id
        }

        try:
            # Timeout ensures no system hang
            response = requests.post(
                url,
                json=payload,
                headers=headers,
                timeout=10
            )

            # Success path: store audio for audit & replay
            if response.status_code == 200:
                timestamp = datetime.utcnow().strftime("%Y%m%d_%H%M%S")
                output_file = f"hazards/voice_{timestamp}.mp3"

                with open(output_file, "wb") as f:
                    f.write(response.content)

                print(f"[VOICE] alert generated → {output_file}")

            # Failure path: log and continue
            else:
                print(
                    f"[VOICE][ERROR] HTTP {response.status_code} — "
                    f"voice skipped safely"
                )

        except Exception as exc:
            # Absolute safety net — NEVER break hazard loop
            print(f"[VOICE][EXCEPTION] {exc}")
```

21:03 i am not sure you review if u want to change give exact place to change # ============================================================
# ReflexAI — LiquidMetal Agent Loop (Orchestration Layer)
# ============================================================
# Purpose:
# --------
# This loop simulates LiquidMetal-style orchestration:
# - Deterministic detectors run on cadence
# - Only meaningful events trigger reasoning
# - Reasoning is governed by RuntimePolicy
#
# This proves ReflexAI is a SYSTEM, not a demo.
# ============================================================

import time
from datetime import datetime

from engine.reasoning_agent import ReasoningAgentFacade
from engine.voice_agent import VoiceAlertAgent
from engine.event_logger import EventLogger


class LiquidMetalAgentLoop:
    """
    Event-driven orchestration loop.

    Responsibilities:
    -----------------
    - Poll perception outputs (simulated here)
    - Detect state changes (events)
    - Invoke governed reasoning ONLY on events
    - Emit a single audit stream
    """

    def __init__(self, health_status: dict | None = None, tick_seconds: float = 0.5):
        self.tick_seconds = tick_seconds
        self.reasoner = ReasoningAgentFacade(health_status=health_status)
        self.last_level = None
        self.voice = VoiceAlertAgent(enabled=True)
        self.logger = EventLogger()


    def run(self, max_ticks: int = 20):
        """
        Run the orchestration loop.

        Parameters:
        -----------
        max_ticks : int
            Safety cap so this never runs forever in demo/tests.
        """
        print("[LIQUIDMETAL] Agent loop starting")
        for tick in range(max_ticks):
            ts = datetime.utcnow().isoformat(timespec="seconds")

            # ------------------------------------------------
            # Simulated perception signal (replace with YOLO)
            # ------------------------------------------------
            perception = self._mock_perception(tick)

            level = perception["level"]

            # ------------------------------------------------
            # Event detection (state change)
            # ------------------------------------------------
            if level != self.last_level:
                print(
                    f"[EVENT] {ts} hazard_level_change "
                    f"{self.last_level} -> {level}"
                )

                reasoning = self.reasoner.reason(perception)
                print(f"[DECISION] {reasoning}")
                self.logger.log({
                    "event": "hazard_decision",
                    "level": reasoning["level"],
                    "confidence": reasoning.get("confidence"),
                    "source": reasoning.get("source"),
                    "policy_mode": self.reasoner.policy.mode
                })

                # Speak only on high risk
                if reasoning["level"] == "DANGER":
                    self.voice.speak(
                        "Danger detected. Operator too close to hazardous zone. Please move back immediately."
                    )
                elif reasoning["level"] == "CAUTION":
                    self.voice.speak(
                        "Caution. Unsafe proximity detected. Maintain safe distance."
                    )


                self.last_level = level

            time.sleep(self.tick_seconds)

        print("[LIQUIDMETAL] Agent loop stopped")

    # --------------------------------------------------------
    # Mock perception source (for orchestration proof)
    # --------------------------------------------------------
    def _mock_perception(self, tick: int) -> dict:
        """
        Cycles through SAFE -> CAUTION -> DANGER deterministically.
        """
        if tick < 5:
            return {
                "hazard": "Worker proximity to robot arm",
                "score": 0.2,
                "level": "SAFE",
            }
        elif tick < 10:
            return {
                "hazard": "Worker proximity to robot arm",
                "score": 0.5,
                "level": "CAUTION",
            }
        else:
            return {
                "hazard": "Worker proximity to robot arm",
                "score": 0.85,
                "level": "DANGER",
            }

                    "policy_mode": self.reasoner.policy.mode
                })

                # Speak only on high risk
                if reasoning["level"] == "DANGER":
                    self.voice.speak(
                        "Danger detected. Operator too close to hazardous zone. Please move back immediately."
                    )
                elif reasoning["level"] == "CAUTION":
                    self.voice.speak(
                        "Caution. Unsafe proximity detected. Maintain safe distance."
                    )


                self.last_level = level

            time.sleep(self.tick_seconds)

        print("[LIQUIDMETAL] Agent loop stopped")

    # --------------------------------------------------------
    # Mock perception source (for orchestration proof)
    # --------------------------------------------------------
    def _mock_perception(self, tick: int) -> dict:
        """
        Cycles through SAFE -> CAUTION -> DANGER deterministically.
        """
        if tick < 5:
            return {
                "hazard": "Worker proximity to robot arm",
                "score": 0.2,
                "level": "SAFE",
            }
        elif tick < 10:
            return {
                "hazard": "Worker proximity to robot arm",
                "score": 0.5,
                "level": "CAUTION",
            }
        else:
            return {
                "hazard": "Worker proximity to robot arm",
                "score": 0.85,
                "level": "DANGER",
            }

"""
Voice Alert Agent — ReflexAI
----------------------------

Purpose:
- Provide optional spoken safety alerts using ElevenLabs TTS.
- Never block or crash the ReflexAI hazard loop.
- Fully policy-gated and audit-safe.

Design principles:
- Voice is a SIDE EFFECT, never a dependency.
- System must work identically with or without ElevenLabs.
- Failures degrade silently and are logged.

Used in:
- LiquidMetal safety loop
- Raindrop SmartComponent demos
- Audit / compliance evidence
"""

import os
import requests
from datetime import datetime
from dotenv import load_dotenv

# Load environment variables from .env (local dev & CI compatible)
load_dotenv()


class VoiceAlertAgent:
    """
    VoiceAlertAgent
    ----------------
    Handles spoken alerts for CAUTION / DANGER events.

    This agent:
    - Uses ElevenLabs Text-to-Speech API
    - Saves generated audio files for audit evidence
    - Never raises exceptions to the caller
    """

    def __init__(self, enabled: bool = True):
        """
        Initialize voice agent.

        Parameters:
        - enabled (bool): Allows runtime policy to turn voice on/off.
        """

        self.enabled = enabled

        # Required ElevenLabs credentials
        self.api_key = os.getenv("ELEVENLABS_API_KEY")
        self.voice_id = os.getenv("ELEVENLABS_VOICE_ID")

        # Optional model override (safe default provided)
        self.model_id = os.getenv(
            "ELEVENLABS_MODEL",
            "eleven_multilingual_v2"
        )

        # Voice agent is READY only if all requirements are met
        self.ready = all([
            self.enabled,
            self.api_key,
            self.voice_id
        ])

        if self.ready:
            print("[VOICE] ElevenLabs voice pipeline ENABLED")
        else:
            print("[VOICE] Voice disabled (missing key, voice_id, or policy)")

    def speak(self, text: str):
        """
        Generate a spoken alert.

        This function:
        - Sends text to ElevenLabs TTS
        - Stores resulting audio locally
        - Never interrupts ReflexAI execution

        Parameters:
        - text (str): Message to be spoken
        """

        # Hard guard: do nothing if voice is unavailable
        if not self.ready:
            return

        # ElevenLabs Text-to-Speech endpoint
        url = f"https://api.elevenlabs.io/v1/text-to-speech/{self.voice_id}"

        # Required headers per ElevenLabs API
        headers = {
            "xi-api-key": self.api_key,
            "Content-Type": "application/json",
            "Accept": "audio/mpeg"
        }

        # Minimal, deterministic payload
        payload = {
            "text": text,
            "model_id": self.model_id
        }

        try:
            # Timeout ensures no system hang
            response = requests.post(
                url,
                json=payload,
                headers=headers,
                timeout=10
            )

            # Success path: store audio for audit & replay
            if response.status_code == 200:
                timestamp = datetime.utcnow().strftime("%Y%m%d_%H%M%S")
                output_file = f"hazards/voice_{timestamp}.mp3"

                with open(output_file, "wb") as f:
                    f.write(response.content)

                print(f"[VOICE] alert generated → {output_file}")

            # Failure path: log and continue
            else:
                print(
                    f"[VOICE][ERROR] HTTP {response.status_code} — "
                    f"voice skipped safely"
                )

        except Exception as exc:
            # Absolute safety net — NEVER break hazard loop
            print(f"[VOICE][EXCEPTION] {exc}")




# ============================================================
# ReflexAI — Event Logger (Offline-first, Cloud-ready)
# ============================================================
# Writes append-only JSONL for audit & dashboards.
# ============================================================

import json
import os
from datetime import datetime

class EventLogger:
    def __init__(self, base_dir="hazards"):
        self.base_dir = base_dir
        os.makedirs(self.base_dir, exist_ok=True)

        day = datetime.utcnow().strftime("%Y-%m-%d")
        self.path = os.path.join(self.base_dir, f"events_{day}.jsonl")

    def log(self, payload: dict):
        record = {
            "ts": datetime.utcnow().isoformat(timespec="seconds"),
            **payload
        }
        with open(self.path, "a", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            f.write(json.dumps(record) + "\n")

On Windows `cmd`, `type` **does not accept paths with `/`**.

You used:

Correct syntax:
```
type engine\voice_agent.py
type engine\event_logger.py
```

## B) Problems we faced (the ones I can **actually** recall from what you pasted)

Here are the concrete ones visible in your logs/messages:

1) **Windows path separator gotcha**  
- `type engine/voice_agent.py` failed because CMD expects `\` not `/`.  
Fix: `type engine\voice_agent.py`.

2) **Scope creep pressure** (many phases at once)  
- CV + Cerebras + LiquidMetal + Voice + Vultr + Dashboard is “6 projects”, not one.  
Result: we locked the pieces that give **real proof** first (voice + logging + loop).

3) **High-risk dependency zone avoided**  
- OpenCV + ONNXRuntime + model files are exactly where Windows environments burn time.

4) **“Oversell wording” vs “what’s implemented” tension**  
- You caught this correctly: claiming batching/parallel without implementing is dangerous.

## H) Phase 5 Vultr: “Can I do in 10 minutes? give exact steps”
Yes — **if we do the simplest possible upload proof**.

### Option 1 (fastest): Upload hazard artifacts to a Vultr VPS with `scp` (10–15 min)
1) Create a Vultr VPS (Ubuntu). Copy the **IP address**.
2) On Windows PowerShell (you already have OpenSSH usually):
```powershell
cd "C:\Users\v_sen\Documents\ChatGpt-Hackthon\Hack0001\ReflexAI-Integrations"
scp -r hazards root@<VPS_IP>:/root/reflexai_hazards
```
3) SSH in:
```powershell
ssh root@<VPS_IP>
ls -la /root/reflexai_hazards
```
That’s already “cloud logging evidence”: your artifacts exist remotely.

### Option 2 (proper “web-view”): Serve artifacts as a tiny web page (extra 5–10 min)
On the VPS:
```bash
cd /root/reflexai_hazards
python3 -m http.server 8080
```
Then open in browser:
`http://<VPS_IP>:8080/`

This is crude but judges can see files.

*(Object Storage is also possible, but S3 keys + tooling usually takes longer than this.)*

---

## I) Dashboard: “vanilla JS vs React — explain clearly (text + minimal code)”

### What you have now
- `dashboard.html` (static page)
- JSONL logs in `hazards/events_YYYY-MM-DD.jsonl`
- mp3 voice files in `hazards/voice_*.mp3`

### What vanilla JS adds (no build tools)
Goal: make dashboard feel “live” by polling logs.

**Minimum live feel (poll every 2s):**
```html
<script>
async function refresh() {
  const res = await fetch('/hazards/events_2025-12-12.jsonl?ts=' + Date.now());
  const text = await res.text();
  const lines = text.trim().split('\n').slice(-30);
  const items = lines.map(l => JSON.parse(l));
  document.getElementById('events').textContent =
    items.map(e => `${e.ts} | ${e.event} | ${e.level} | ${e.policy_mode}`).join('\n');
}
setInterval(refresh, 2000);
refresh();
</script>

<pre id="events">Loading…</pre>
```

**Important:** `fetch('/hazards/...')` works only if you serve the folder via HTTP:
```bash
python -m http.server 8000
```
Then open: `http://localhost:8000/dashboard.html`

### What React adds (why it’s slower)
React is mainly for:
- components (cards, charts)
- better state management
- timeline chart library

But it needs:
- Node + npm
- Vite setup
- build + deploy steps
So: React = prettier, not necessary for proof.

- give you a **drop-in dashboard.html patch** that auto-refreshes and links voice mp3 files.

## Memory Export — Failure-Aware Technical Summary  
*(for inclusion into 🚀 Hackathon – 002.07 – ReflexAI Closure, Failure Log & Outcome Synthesis)*

---

### 1️⃣ What was attempted on this page

- Phase-2 verification of ReflexAI as a **trust-layered, SaaS-aware system**, not a demo.
- Prove **hybrid reasoning routing** (live SaaS vs fallback).
- Validate **environment isolation** (`.env` vs CMD vars).
- Integrate and verify **ElevenLabs voice alerts**.
- Establish **audit-grade local logging** of hazard events.
- Build a **basic web dashboard** to visualize runtime events.
- Explore feasibility of **Phase 5 (Vultr logging)** and **Phase 6 (Raindrop dashboard)** without breaking stability.
- Stress-test assumptions about “how much can realistically be done” in hackathon conditions.

---

### 2️⃣ What actually worked (verified)

- **Hybrid reasoning path**
  - `ReasoningAgentFacade` correctly routed between fallback and “live-ready” modes.
  - Health checks and policy gating worked as designed.
  - Proof via runtime logs showing `reasoning=fallback / hybrid` without crashes.

- **LiquidMetal-style orchestration loop**
  - Event-driven loop triggered reasoning only on state changes.
  - Non-blocking design confirmed (voice + logging did not halt loop).
  - Deterministic mock perception produced stable, reproducible runs.

- **ElevenLabs voice integration (REAL SaaS)**
  - Root cause of 401 errors identified: missing permissions + invalid `voice_id` usage.
  - Correct API usage achieved with real `VOICE_ID`.
  - Successful TTS call confirmed (`Status: 200`, `test.mp3` generated).
  - Final `VoiceAlertAgent` design:
    - Policy-gated
    - Failure-safe
    - Stores audio evidence (`hazards/voice_*.mp3`)
  - This was the strongest, fully proven SaaS integration.

- **Audit-grade local logging**
  - `EventLogger` wrote append-only JSONL files.
  - Verified contents with timestamps, confidence, source, policy mode.
  - Logs suitable for later analytics or cloud upload.

- **Basic web dashboard**
  - `dashboard.html` renders event cards from runtime JSON.
  - Confirmed it is a web page (HTML/JS), not a desktop app.
  - Runtime data pipeline exists (`events → JSON → dashboard`).

---

### 3️⃣ What failed or caused friction

- **Vision / YOLO integration**
  - No real YOLO inference wired into the decision loop.
  - Perception remained `_mock_perception()` throughout.
  - ONNX/OpenCV/CPU pipeline on Windows was not attempted due to high failure risk.

- **Dashboard “live” feeling**
  - Initial dashboard used hardcoded JS data during debugging.
  - Runtime refresh required manual regeneration (`prepare_dashboard_data.py`).
  - No auto-polling or live updates implemented.
  - Result felt “toy-like” despite correct data flow.

- **ElevenLabs setup friction**
  - Significant time lost due to:
    - Confusion between `voices/settings/default` vs actual `voice_id`
    - Missing `voices_read` permission
    - Silent 401 failures
  - Required multiple minimal test scripts to isolate the issue.

- **Windows + hackathon tooling**
  - Command-line friction (`type` vs path issues).
  - Python environment churn.
  - Repeated context switching slowed execution.

---

### 4️⃣ Incorrect assumptions identified

- **AI (planning side)**
  - Overestimated how much could be safely executed on:
    - Windows
    - CPU-only
    - Single developer
    - Hackathon time window
  - Treated YOLO + OpenCV as “medium effort” when it is actually **high-risk** in this environment.
  - Oversold Phase labels (e.g., “wins categories”) relative to actual implementation depth.

- **Human (execution side)**
  - Trusted that all advertised hackathon credits/tools would be quick to activate.
  - Assumed cloud logging (Vultr) would be trivial once local logging worked.
  - Stayed in “robot mode” longer than optimal when architectural pushback was warranted.

---

### 5️⃣ Decisions that materially affected later pages

- **Explicit decision to skip Phase 1 (YOLO real perception)**
  - Preserved a working, demonstrable system.
  - Avoided likely environment-breaking failures.
  - Forced honest framing of “mock perception”.

- **Decision to lock ElevenLabs once it worked**
  - Prevented regression.
  - Provided concrete SaaS proof for submission.

- **Decision to keep logging local-first**
  - Enabled audit evidence without cloud risk.
  - Deferred Vultr as optional.

- **Decision to create a new page (002.06) for honest submission**
  - Separated execution from narrative.
  - Allowed technical truth without hype.

---

### 6️⃣ Net outcome of Page 002.05

- Produced **real, verifiable SaaS evidence** (voice + logs).
- Exposed the **gap between architectural ambition and execution reality**.
- Forced a reset toward **honest technical disclosure** instead of feature chasing.
- Became the factual backbone for closure and failure analysis in Page 002.07.

### 3) Windows CLI friction: `type` command failed on subpaths
- **Start:** 12/12 21:39  
- **End:** 12/12 21:42  
- **Duration:** **3 minutes**
- **Steps executed (observed):**
  1. `notepad engine/voice_agent.py`
  2. `type engine/voice_agent.py` → **The syntax of the command is incorrect**
  3. `type engine/event_logger.py` → **The syntax of the command is incorrect**
  4. `notepad engine/event_logger.py`
- **Root cause (precise):**
  - In Windows CMD, `type` expects Windows path separators. Your command used `/`.
  - Correct commands:
    - `type engine\voice_agent.py`
    - `type engine\event_logger.py`
    - Or safer: `type "engine\voice_agent.py"`
- **Outcome:** ❌ “type failed” due to slash usage, not code failure.
